<meta>This page is the title page for a work by Julius Sperber. Although the previous page contained errata for what was likely a preceding document, this page marks the beginning of a specific treatise on eschatology (the study of the end times). Such compilations were common in the 17th century, where multiple related pamphlets were bound together.</meta>

->A Secret<-

# TREATISE
<note>original Latin: *TRACTATUS*</note>

## BY JULIUS SPERBER
<note>original Latin: *IULII SPERBERI*</note>

->Concerning the three<-

# A G E S
<note>original Latin: *S E C U L I S*, referring to the great epochs of human history</note>

->or<-

## Principal Epochs, from the Beginning
## to the End of the World.

In which, specifically from the Word of God, it is clearly demonstrated that a Golden Age—as the third and final era—is still remaining <note>original: *hinderstellig*, implying an era that is yet to be realized or is outstanding in the divine plan</note>, and what the condition of the same shall be.

# PREFACE

->To the<-

## CHRISTIAN READER.

The ancients have said it very well: *Distinguish the periods of time and the Scripture will harmonize* <note>original Latin: *Distingue Tempora & concordabit Scriptura*. This was a common theological maxim suggesting that apparent contradictions in the Bible are resolved when one understands which era or "dispensation" the text refers to.</note>; had this always been done, then not so many conflicting interpretations would have come to light. For if we watch with enlightened eyes what has been fulfilled until now, what is presently occurring, and what is yet to come in the future—that which is first to be fulfilled—then all prophetic and apostolic writings will soon be revealed to us. Likewise, if we take note of how everything prefigured in the Old Testament, and acquired for us, gifted, and actually shared with us through faith in the New Testament through Christ, shall now soon be transformed into vision <note>vision: The German word *Schawen* (beholding) refers to the direct, intuitive experience of God’s presence, as opposed to knowing Him only through faith or written words.</note> and be revealed in us, then we shall clearly see how the <term>Mystery of God</term> <note>A biblical concept, specifically from Revelation 10:7, referring to God's secret plan for the end of the world and the salvation of humanity.</note> proclaimed through the prophets is only now truly to be completed. Everything that God spoke through the prophets from the beginning is now to be restored <note>German: *herwieder gebracht*. This is a reference to the "restoration of all things" (Apokatastasis) mentioned in Acts 3:21.</note>—that is, to be revealed according to vision, just as we have hitherto believed it and expected it in patient hope (Acts 3, Revelation 10, Hebrews 6). So precisely does the entirety of Holy Scripture aim at the final time, and it now stands in full fulfillment, in that all punishments, threats,

<summary>The author introduces a method for understanding the Bible by distinguishing between different historical and prophetic eras, arguing that the "Mystery of God" and the "restoration of all things" are nearing their final fulfillment.</summary>
<keywords>hermeneutics, prophecy, restoration of all things, biblical interpretation, eschatology, Old Testament, New Testament</keywords>

# PREFACE
->To the<-
## CHRISTIAN READER.

<image-desc>Decorative initial 'E' within a square frame, embellished with intricate white vine-work patterns against a dark, hatched background.</image-desc>

The ancients have said quite rightly: *Distinguish the times and the Scripture will agree* <note>original Latin: *Distingue Tempora & concordabit Scriptura*</note>. Had this always been done, so many contradictory interpretations would not have come to light; for if we do not observe with enlightened eyes that which has been fulfilled until now, that which is currently present, and that which is to come in the future—which is yet to be fulfilled—then all Prophetic and Apostolic Scriptures will soon be made plain to us. Likewise, if we take note of how everything that was prefigured in the Old Testament, and earned for us, granted to us, and effectively shared through faith in the New Testament through Christ, is now soon to be transformed into sight <term>Sight (German: Schauen): In this mystical context, it refers to the direct, "face-to-face" experience of divine truth, contrasted with the indirect nature of faith.</term> and shall be revealed in us, then we will see clearly how the mystery of God <term>Mystery of God: Referring to God's hidden plan for the salvation and restoration of the world, as mentioned in the Book of Revelation.</term> announced through the prophets is only now to be truly completed. And everything that God has spoken through the prophets from the beginning shall now be restored—that is, revealed through sight—just as we have hitherto believed it and expected it in patient hope (Acts 3; Revelation 10; Hebrews 6 <note>original citations: Actor. 3. Apoc. 10. Hebr. 6.</note>). Indeed, the whole of Holy Scripture aims at the end times and now stands in full fulfillment, in which all of those punishments, threats,

<summary>This preface introduces a theological argument for a chronological interpretation of the Bible, suggesting that the "Mystery of God" is nearing its final revelation. The author emphasizes that understanding the distinction between past, present, and future fulfillments is the key to resolving contradictions in scriptural interpretation.</summary>
<keywords>hermeneutics, biblical prophecy, end times, restoration, faith vs sight, mystery of God, interpretation</keywords>

->P R E F A C E<-

Curses and plagues shall now be poured out upon the godless and un-
believing; conversely, all **promises**, **blessings**, **rewards of grace**, and **blessedness** shall be revealed and made true for the believers. We see the physical redemption, rest, kingdom, and prosperity in the Jews of the Old Testament; the spiritual redemption, rest, kingdom of grace, and blessedness out of the kingdom of Satan, we have also already received through Christ; but in this we believe the Word, because we do not yet see it revealed in us, 1 Corinthians 13.

Now there still remains the revelation of that which we have believed, when we—spiritually and physically redeemed from this <term>Babylon and Egypt</term> <note>The author uses these biblical locations metaphorically to represent the state of sin and worldly bondage from which the soul must escape.</note>—shall enter into eternal rest, peace, joy, and blessedness in the coming **Kingdom of Glory** with Christ, Hebrews 3, Romans 8, 1 Peter 1, 2 Thessalonians 1, Colossians 3. And just as every redemption has brought the destruction of their enemies to the children of God, so also this present redemption will cause the destruction of all our enemies; our day of rest and redemption will be a day of vengeance and imprisonment for them, and our <term>Year of Jubilee</term> <note>In ancient Hebrew law, the Year of Jubilee (every 50 years) was a time when debts were forgiven and slaves set free. Here it refers to the final deliverance of believers at the end of time.</note> will be for them a year of eternal anguish and torment, Isaiah 2, 11, 34, 61, 63, 65; Exodus 15; Luke 21; Daniel 7; Jeremiah 50, 51; Psalm 37.

Therefore, the remaining life of the believers after **Baptism** in this world is always called by the Apostles an "expectation of the revelation and appearance of the glory of the Lord Jesus," 1 Corinthians 1, Titus 2, 2 Thessalonians 1. For just as He once appeared in His <term>state of humiliation</term> <note>A theological term (*status exinanitionis*) referring to the period of Christ's life on earth where He set aside His divine majesty to live as a servant and suffer.</note> and brought His Kingdom of Grace near to us—when He sacrificed Himself to God for the removal of our sins and redeemed us from Satan’s kingdom through Himself—so it is still to come in the future that He—with His Kingdom of Glory and prepared blessedness—shall reveal Himself from heaven according to the <term>state of His exaltation</term> <note>The *status exaltationis*, referring to Christ's return to His full divine glory and power after the resurrection.</note>, restoring all things, making our spiritual and physical enemies completely subject to Him, sharing all His glory with us, and

It emphasizes that the believer's life is a period of waiting for the final defeat of enemies and the restoration of all things.</summary>

...as a head [He shall transfer] us, His members, with Him into His eternal **rest**, **refreshment**, and **blessedness**, both here in time and there in eternity. See <note>original Latin: "Vid."</note> Hebrews 2, 3, 4, 9, 10, 12; 1 Peter 1; 2 Timothy 4; 2 Thessalonians 1, 2; 1 Corinthians 15; Luke 19, 21; Matthew 19, 24, 25; Acts 1, 3.

Of this appearing and coming of Christ—intended for the restoration of the Kingdom of Israel and the <term>restitution of all things</term> <note>The "restitution of all things" (Greek: apokatastasis) refers to the theological belief that all of creation will eventually be restored to a state of perfection.</note>—the Pharisees, Sadducees, and Essenes of today <note>The author is using these ancient Jewish sects as metaphors for the various types of modern theologians and clergy he considers blind or corrupt.</note> know just as little as they knew in Christ’s time about His coming of grace and the spiritual rebirth. To them, these matters are nothing but hidden mysteries and blasphemies. In this regard, they are truly blind and "blind leaders of the blind," who lead all their listeners away from Christ and His coming—which brings eternal redemption and the rebirth of His children—and instead toward their old temple rituals and false worship. They do this just as the Jews did back then, believing that their modern-day Jerusalem (in which they have long since crucified and rooted out Christ much more cruelly than the Jews did) should remain standing forever. Malachi 3; Isaiah 29, 42, 58; Jeremiah 7, 23; Matthew 15, 23; John 8, 9, and following <note>original Latin: "& seqq."</note>.

Now, we have lived to see these **sorrowful times**, just as they were foretold to us by Christ and His apostles. Matthew 24, 25; Luke 18, 19, 21; 2 Timothy 3, 4; 2 Peter 2, 3. If only we would truly take their danger to heart, keep watch, pray, adapt ourselves to the present time, <term>depart from Babylon</term> <note>In this context, "Babylon" represents the state of spiritual confusion and the corrupt worldly church from which the believer must flee.</note>, and lift up our heads toward our redemption! This way, we might not perish in the wrath of the Lord along with those complacent scoffers who have always been blind to the present state of things from the very beginning. 2 Peter 2, 3.

However, because there are great disputes and doubts among many concerning the times to come and what yet awaits the world—even though it is all described clearly and distinctly in God’s Word, if only our eyes were opened so that we might see it, believe with simplicity, and not follow our own imagined opinions more than God’s Word (our lamp in this darkness)—I wish to provide you, Christian reader, with a brief guide to the knowledge of future things. The further explanation of these...

...and proof you will find in this present book.

For **I.** it is indeed manifest and without doubt that the judgment upon the Whore and upon Babylon <note>In the context of the Reformation and radical pietism, "the Whore" and "Babylon" were frequently used as metaphors for the perceived corruption of the institutional Church, specifically the Papacy or any state-sanctioned religious system.</note> is at the door, Revelation 17, 18, which shall awaken great joy and exultation among the saints.

**II.** Then Christ shall assume His full authority, power, and dominion, and shall set out with His saints and heavenly host for His people, that He may fight against the kings of the earth, overcome them, slay them, tread them down in His wrath, crush them, slaughter them, and give their flesh to the birds of heaven to devour, Psalm 2, 110, 149; Isaiah 3, 34; Ezekiel 38, 39; Daniel 2, 7, 12; Luke 19; Revelation 12, 17, 19; Joel 3. This is the fierce day of vengeance of the Lord, of which all the prophets have prophesied, which shall be a heartfelt day of redemption for the children of God, Luke 21; Isaiah 11, 13, 34, 61, 63; Malachi 3; Zephaniah 1, 2; Zechariah 14; Joel 2, 3; Jeremiah 50, 51; Malachi 3, 4.

When the Lord shall thus manifest Himself so gloriously before the world, then thirdly (**III.**), the Jews shall also be awakened and, together with the remaining Gentiles who turn to the Lord with their whole heart, be taken back into the eternal covenant of God, so that one Church shall be formed from Jews and Gentiles under the single head, Christ, John 10; Ezekiel 16, 34, 36, 37, 39; Jeremiah 31, 32, 33; Hosea 14; Amos 9; Micah 4, 5, 7; Zephaniah 3; Joel 3; Zechariah 1, 2, 8, 9, 10, and following <note>original Latin: "seq."</note>.

**IV.** This, His spiritual body, Christ shall—after such a heartfelt awakening from the grave—exalt in and with Himself, glorify, and lead into His Paradise of rest and refreshment, so that they may celebrate the true <term>Sabbath</term> <note>The "true Sabbath" here refers to an eternal state of spiritual rest and peace for believers after the trials of the world.</note> and <term>Jubilee year</term> <note>The Jubilee year was an ancient Hebrew tradition of debt cancellation and liberation, here used to symbolize the ultimate liberation of the soul.</note> here in spiritual joy with Him, and reign with Him for a thousand years in the fellowship of His honor, glory, might, power, judgment, kingdom, and dominion over the Gentiles, Revelation 2, 20; Daniel 7; 1 Corinthians 15.

This exaltation of the Church of Christ upon this earth, Revelation 5, to equal power and dominion over all her enemies, and to the kingdom, priesthood, and royal office with Christ her Head, is prefigured for us in

->P R E F A C E<-

...to the Jubilee year <note>A reference to the *Annus Iubilaeus* in the Old Testament, a year of release from debt and bondage occurring every fifty years.</note> of the Old Testament and in the forty days that Christ walked here on Earth after His Resurrection; and it will be so much more joyful and secure, because the Devil, the Beast, and the false prophet with their company will be cast down into Hell and sealed therein, Revelation 19, 20.

Thereupon follows **V.** after a thousand years the surrounding and siege of the beloved city by the remaining Gentiles <note>The original German uses *Heiden* (heathens), referring to the nations led by Gog and Magog.</note> from the four corners of the earth, whom God will consume and shatter with fire, Revelation 20; Micah 4, 5, 7; Isaiah 41; Zechariah 1, 2, 8, 9, 10, 12, 14; Joel 3.

**VI.** Then finally comes the Last General Judgment, the handing over of Christ’s Kingdom to His Father, and our entry into eternal life, glory, and blessedness, when GOD shall be all in all, 1 Corinthians 15; Revelation 20, 21, 22; Matthew 25.

If you take this order to heart and accordingly test everything diligently against the Word of God, and believe and accept it as far as it accords with God's Word, then you will be able to derive great benefit from this book, and it will richly reward your labor and expense.

But if there be anyone who does not grasp this prophetic light, let him nonetheless not despise the well-intended work of this enlightened man, lest he otherwise despise God and His Spirit; for throughout, only Holy Scripture (and indeed, Scripture explained by Scripture) is cited here, and as it were, a beautiful wreath is woven from so many divine flowers of Paradise <note>original: *Paradeises-blümlein*</note>, so that one reader here, another there, will without doubt perceive a sweet fragrance and lovely taste therein.

I pray meanwhile and herewith kindly request the reader of this book that, if perhaps some of this enlightened man’s heartfelt writings might be found in his possession, he would [send] them to me...

...many beautiful flowers of Scripture <note>This continues the sentence from the previous page: "...a beautiful wreath is woven from so many flowers of Scripture."</note>. If anyone possesses more such manuscripts by this author, it is requested that they be willing to share them <note>The original German *communiciren* implies both sharing and distributing for the purpose of publication.</note> for a fair price, so that they may be brought to print for the general benefit. For it is truly irresponsible to hide such a light under a bushel, but rather it should be set upon a candlestick, so that it may shine and give light to all those within the house <note>A direct reference to the Parable of the Lamp in Matthew 5:15, emphasizing the moral duty to publish spiritual insights.</note>. Done in Amsterdam, the 22nd of July, in the year 1660.

# PREFACE
->To the<-
## CHRISTIAN READER.

<margin>
Amos 3:7.
Hosea 12:10.
Isaiah 56:10.
</margin>
GOD the LORD has generally granted His Saints and Prophets this great advantage above other people: that He let them know and see in their own time those things which were perhaps to occur and happen much later, after a very long time.

<margin>
Ezekiel 3:17.
</margin>
Therefore, the Prophets in the Scriptures were also called <term>Speculatores</term> <note>original: "Speculatores" - Latin for watchmen, observers, or scouts.</note> by GOD, which is to say, Seers. However, such seeing did not happen with physical eyes, but with the spiritual eyes of the mind. This way of seeing is well known to those who are endowed with the Wisdom of God, or who are well-versed in that same <term>Studio</term> <note>original: "Studio" - here referring to a specific field of study, spiritual discipline, or pursuit.</note>.

<margin>
Chrysostom
on Genesis,
Homily 17.
</margin>
Of such Seers, the ancient Teacher Chrysostom <note>John Chrysostom (c. 347–407 AD), an influential Early Church Father and Archbishop of Constantinople known for his eloquent preaching.</note> has made some mention, where he writes with these words: <term>Prophetæ mentalibus oculis futura viderunt</term> <note>Latin: "The prophets saw future things with the eyes of the mind."</note>, that is: The Prophets of God saw the fu- <note>The text breaks here due to the page ending; the catchword "künff-" suggests the next word is "künftigen," meaning "future things."</note>

<summary>This page introduces a preface regarding the nature of prophecy, explaining that God grants prophets the "spiritual eyes" to see future events long before they happen. It cites biblical prophets and the Church Father John Chrysostom to define prophets as spiritual "watchmen."</summary>
<keywords>prophecy, Seers, John Chrysostom, spiritual vision, Speculatores, biblical commentary, watchmen</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the "Preface to the Christian Reader" from the previous page, finishing the sentence regarding the Church Father Chrysostom's description of prophetic vision. It establishes a theological argument that prophets see future events through spiritual enlightenment rather than physical sight, citing examples from both the Old and New Testaments.</meta>

# PREFACE

future things with spiritual eyes, or with the eyes of the mind. <margin>Ephesians 1:18.</margin> This is what the Apostle Paul calls the *illuminated eyes of the heart* <note>original Latin: "illuminatos oculos cordis"</note>, that is, enlightened eyes of the heart. With such eyes, so many hundreds of years before, Abraham had also seen the day <margin>John 8:56</margin> of the **LORD** Christ and rejoiced in it, as Christ said in the New Testament. Thus <margin>Acts 7:55, 56.</margin> Stephen also saw, when he became full of the Holy Spirit, the glory of **GOD**, and **JESUS** standing at the right hand of **GOD**.

Therefore, the prophecies of the prophets described in Holy Scripture are not only comforting teachings and sermons of rebuke; rather, they also proclaim such things beforehand that are to occur, happen, and follow a long time afterward. In particular, many glorious prophecies concerning the condition of the future and final times are contained therein. As then, especially and above the others, the Prophet Isaiah <note>original: "Esaias"</note> prophesied much about the last time of the world and the condition of the New Jerusalem: of whom Sirach <note>Referring to the Book of Sirach, also known as Ecclesiasticus, in the Apocrypha.</note> wrote that he prophesied with <margin>Sirach 48:27.</margin> a rich spirit what should happen at the last: and that he [comforted] the—

<summary>This page explains that biblical prophecy is a form of spiritual "seeing" granted by God, allowing prophets like Abraham, Isaiah, and Stephen to perceive future or divine realities through the 'eyes of the heart.'</summary>
<keywords>prophecy, spiritual vision, eyes of the heart, Abraham, Stephen, Isaiah, Sirach, New Jerusalem, eschatology</keywords>

# PREFACE

afflicted in Zion.

However, among many who do not rightly understand the Scriptures, it often appears as if the prophets and their prophecies were frequently contradictory to one another. Therefore, one finds such people who, for this reason, consider them altogether null and untrue. Of this we now have an example in Scripture in King Zedekiah:

<margin>
Jeremiah 39:7.
Jeremiah 32:3, and following.
</margin>
to him the prophet Jeremiah had prophesied that he would be overcome by the Babylonian king, seized, and delivered into his hand; that he would see him with his own eyes, speak with him face to face, and would come to Babylon.

<margin>
Ezekiel 12:13, and following.
</margin>
But the prophet Ezekiel had prophesied to him that he (King Zedekiah) would not see the city of Babylon. The aforementioned King Zedekiah considered these two kinds of prophecies to be contradictory <note>original: "wiederwertig," meaning opposing or repugnant to reason</note>, and therefore also held them to be uncertain. He wished to give credit to the one as little as to the other.

However, afterward, belief was unfortunately forced upon him by the outcome <note>literally: "faith came into his hands," an idiom meaning he was forced to believe by the reality of the events</note>, and both prophecies came true: For he was [brought] before the Babylo—

<meta>catchword: nian</meta>

<summary>This page addresses the skepticism of those who find biblical prophecies contradictory, using the historical example of King Zedekiah. Zedekiah doubted the prophets Jeremiah and Ezekiel because their predictions seemed to clash, though both ultimately proved true when he was captured and blinded.</summary>

</margin>
—nian King Nebuchadnezzar <note>original: "Nabuchodonasar"</note> as a prisoner, whom he saw with his own eyes and spoke with, just as Jeremiah had prophesied to him. However, after his eyes were put out before he was even brought to Babylon, he certainly did not see the city of Babylon, even though he was brought there as a captive. Thus, Ezekiel’s prophecy was also truly fulfilled in him—namely, that he would not see the city of Babylon. Therefore, it remains undoubtedly and undeniably 

<margin>
Augustine, *On the Harmony of the Evangelists*, Book 3. Acts 3:18. Also 2 Peter 1:12.
</margin>
true, as Saint Augustine wrote, that the prophets all prophesied together from one Spirit. For this reason, their prophecies—as those which God the LORD Himself spoke through their mouths—are true and certain in their proper understanding, because the Word of God is and can be nothing but the truth. On the other hand, no value is to be placed on any prophecy that has sprung from human brains and does not come through the inspiration of the Most High, as the wise man Sirach <note>original: "Syrach," also known as the book of Ecclesiasticus</note> admonishes.

<margin>
Sirach 34:5, 6. Matthew 7:15, 16. 1 John 4:1.
</margin>
Therefore, we should take this lesson and warning: that we guard ourselves against such prophets and also test the spirits to see whether they are from God.

# P R E F A C E.

—should: which cannot be done more appropriately or better than when one compares their prophecies against the Word of God. To the extent that they are consistent and in harmony with it, to that extent one shall and must allow them to pass as certain and right. Even those prophecies which are not so precisely or explicitly founded in God’s Word—at least according to the literal sense—yet do not run contrary to it and originate from God-enlightened, blessed individuals: though one may not always hold them to be entirely correct and certain, they should still be allowed to keep their value and remain uncriticized. However, if anyone should dare to bring forward such prophecies that openly run contrary to the Word of God and its actual, correct understanding, these should rightly be weeded out and rejected as false and untrue.

<margin>

# P R E F A C E.

—be consistent with Holy Scripture and the Word of GOD. Indeed, many—even among Christians (to say nothing here of the heathens and unbelievers)—have for some time now presumed to prophesy and foretell how things shall go in this world regarding one matter or another until the future Day of the LORD <note>The "Day of the Lord" refers to the end of the world and the final judgment.</note>. Some have done so based on the course of the heavenly stars <note>This refers to judicial astrology, the practice of predicting earthly events based on planetary movements.</note>, others through all sorts of calculations of specific years and times, and still others based on different grounds, or even, in part, without any basis at all.

It is not at all my intention here to examine this or that prophecy, but rather to leave them in their place.

However, such *authors* <note>original Latin: *Auctores*</note>, and all others who have written of such things until now, have primarily and solely looked at the time—namely, how things shall proceed shortly before the second Coming of Christ, and thus until the Day of the LORD. But no one has *specifically* <note>original Latin: *in specie*</note> described what the state of affairs will be regarding various matters once the Lord Christ has come for the second time and the Day of the LORD has dawned: except that some

# P R E F A C E.

I, however, have now undertaken this same work—certainly not in a lighthearted manner, nor out of any presumed conceit of special art <note>original: *Kunſt*</note> and knowledge <note>original: *Wiſſenſchafft*</note>. And because these are things that have not been treated by anyone before, I am therefore all the more certain that when this becomes known to many people, I shall have enough critics <note>original: *Cenſores*</note> and wise-acres <note>original: *Klüglinge*; this term refers to people who pride themselves on being cleverer than others, often in a pedantic or dismissive way</note> regarding this matter, who would presume to find fault with this work of mine in all sorts of ways and means, as if it were a new and previously unheard-of teaching <note>original: *Leh-re*</note>.

However, I let this hinder me very little: because I have by no means undertaken the description of these things for the sake of such people. Rather, this has been undertaken solely for the comfort and benefit of certain godly <note>original: *Gottſeeligen*</note> and peace-loving <note>original: *Friedliebenden*</note> people, who have a truly yearning and eager desire for the coming golden age <note>original: *güldenen Zeit*; a reference to a predicted era of peace and spiritual perfection on earth</note> and the eternal life of joy <note>original: *Frewden-leben*</note>. It does not follow from this that, because of those events, all people here in this world will rise and gather toward Jerusalem. <margin>4 Esdras 9:5.</margin>
It is said to Esdras by the angel: "Just as everything that is made in the world has a beginning and an end, and its end is manifest; so too the eras of the Most High have a manifest beginning, with wonders and powers, and they conclude with deeds and signs." <note>This quote is from the apocryphal book of 2 Esdras (also known as 4 Ezra), which was often cited in mystical and apocalyptic literature of this period.</note> Now, I consider it such that (especially among us Christians) no one would be so irrational as to doubt that we are now in a different *Seculo* <note>original: *Seculo*; Latin for 'Age' or 'World-Era'</note>, and thus in a different time, than that which existed previously in the Old Testament. Therefore, one can also perceive, particularly from the reasons cited above, that just as there are three distinct Persons in the Godhead, so too, according to these Persons and their offices and duties, there are three distinct Ages or eras that follow one after another. For the first era, which is attributed to God the Father, we should and must rightly consider the time of the Law or the Old Testament, and as long as the people were bound to keep the same.

<margin>Galatians 4:4.</margin>

...his servant Moses on Mount Sinai, where, alongside other mysteries, He also showed him the <term>secrets of the times</term> <note>original: *Secreta temporum*</note>, the hidden things and the end of the ages. We now intend to examine these three periods, and shall call the first the <term>Age of Nature</term> <note>original: *Seculum Naturæ*</note>, the second the <term>Age of Grace</term> <note>original: *Seculum Gratiæ*</note>, and the third the <term>Age of Glory</term> <note>original: *Seculum Gloriæ*</note>.

At this point, someone might further ask: what is the nature of the distinction between these three times? How and in what manner does one follow the other? Is there perhaps an empty space between them before another time begins again? Regarding such 
<margin>4 Ezra 9:8 & following.</margin> matters and the determination of the eras, Esdras <note>the prophet Ezra, traditionally associated with apocalyptic visions</note> was likewise troubled. Then **GOD** gave him to understand, through the mystery of Esau and Jacob <note>referring to the twin brothers in Genesis; Jacob was born holding Esau's heel, symbolizing immediate succession</note>, that one time hangs upon another, and thus the future age also hangs upon the present one, following immediately after it, just as Jacob held Esau by 
<margin>Chapter 7:43.</margin> the heel when he was born. Therefore, the **Day of Judgment** shall be the end of this time and the beginning of future immortality. Indeed, the eras follow one another just like day and night; for just as the morning star is an end of the night and a beginning of the day, and likewise as the evening is an end of the day and a beginning of the night, even so do the three <term>ages</term> <note>original: *Secula*</note> or eras follow directly one after another.

<margin>Hosea 6:2, 3.</margin> The prophet Hosea looked toward this when he prophesied in the Spirit: The **LORD** makes us alive after two days; He will raise us up on the third day (which is in the third age), so that we shall live before Him.

As David also says in his last words: Like the light of the morning, when the sun rises; for when that same dawn breaks, then no night shall follow any longer. That is: after that time, there will be no more time or distinction of times, but rather the end and eternal life. For just as the times first took their certain beginning, so they shall also finally take their departure and end. And just as before the beginning there was neither time nor hour, neither day nor year, so after the end of the times there will be no more time or distinction of years, days, hours, or seasons. Instead, just as before there was always an everlasting eternity without any beginning, so hereafter, after the ending of the times, there will again be a continuous, everlasting eternity without end or ceasing, and it shall remain constant. Thus, the three times or <term>Great Ages</term> <note>original: *Haupt-secula*</note> that have now been mentioned are, as it were, a very small <term>interstice</term> <note>original: *instertitium*, likely a printer's error for *interstitium*</note> or <term>interval</term> <note>original: *intervallum*</note> within the eternity that continues forever; and in the eyes of **GOD** in Heaven, they are hardly to be counted as more than a week might be to us humans in the world.

-> Erste Theil. <-

[...miraculous signs had] been [present] in the same person, [being] <term>self-communicating <note>original Latin: *communicativus sui*; a theological term describing how the Holy Spirit or a divine gift can be shared or passed from one person to another without being diminished</note></term>, as it was in these three holy men and prophets? For, firstly, regarding Moses, the Scripture says:

<margin>Numbers 11:17, 25.</margin>
God took from the Spirit of Moses and gave it to the seventy men, who thereafter prophesied and bore the burden of governance alongside Moses. Likewise, it is written of the Lord Christ in the New Testament that He breathed upon His disciples and said to them:

<margin>John 20:22.</margin>
"Receive the Holy Spirit." Therefore the Apostle Paul writes: God has sent the Spirit of His Son into our hearts, who cries within us: "Abba, dear Father!"

<margin>Galatians 4:6.</margin>
Indeed, Christ dwells in all believers and the elect; so much so, that whoever does not have the Spirit of Christ does not belong to Him.

<margin>Romans 8:10, 11.</margin>
Finally, regarding the prophet Elijah, the Scripture testifies explicitly: That the Spirit of Elijah, as he ascended toward Heaven, came upon his disciple Elisha, as he had requested.

<margin>4 Kings 2:9 & 5. <note>In modern English Bibles, this is the book of 2 Kings</note></margin>
And in the New Testament, Luke writes that John the Baptist would go before the Lord in the spirit and power of Elijah.

<margin>Luke 1:17. VII.</margin>
Seventhly, apart from these three aforementioned persons, one finds no other single human being in the Holy Divine Scripture who is known to have ascended to Heaven with both body and soul. For although it is written of Enoch that God "took him away" and he was seen no more, it does not yet follow from this that he ascended to Heaven with body and soul. Regarding Moses, it is indeed written in the Scriptures that he died and was buried by the Lord; however, the text explicitly mentions that, nonetheless, no one has ever discovered his grave. As such, the Saint Ambrose and other Church Fathers were of the opinion that Moses did not die, but was instead carried away <note>original: *verzucket*; suggests a rapturous or supernatural removal</note> into Paradise to a better life. The reliable Jewish historian Josephus publicly testifies: That although Moses wrote in his books for specific reasons that he had died, he was actually suddenly surrounded by a cloud and taken away in a valley. In this same way, the Lord Christ was also taken up by a cloud and ascended into Heaven. Likewise, the prophet Elijah ascended to Heaven in a whirlwind <note>original: *Wetter*; can mean weather, storm, or whirlwind</note> upon a chariot of fire.

### The Eleventh Chapter. ###

### Threefold Sacrament or Sign of the Covenant. ###

<margin>Augustine, Letter 4.</margin> **Holy Augustine** writes in one place <note>original: "an einem orth"; likely referring to a specific passage in his Epistles</note> that the changing of the sacraments was prophesied beforehand by the prophets: Regarding which it is to be known, that such a change in the Church of God was never undertaken in a disorderly or reckless manner; nor did any such change ever occur outside of a time when other significant matters were also changed. When this happens, then truly another *Seculum* <term>Age or World-Era</term> begins.

## The Thirteenth Chapter.

### Three Types of Theology or Religions.

<margin>Acts 20:24, Eph. 1:13, Luke 16:16, Rom. 1:9, Rom. 15:19, Rev. 14:6</margin>
In the time of the Father, in the first <term>age</term> <note>original: "Seculo." Here meaning a specific epoch or era of time.</note> and Testament, there was the Jewish Theology or Religion—namely, the Law. In the time of the Son, in the New Testament, we have had until now, and still have, the Christian Theology or Religion—namely, the Gospel of the Grace of **God** and of our salvation. Concerning both of these religions and how they follow one after the other, the **Lord** Christ speaks with express words: "The Law and the Prophets prophesied until John; and from that time on, the Gospel of the Kingdom of **God** is preached." Therefore, the Apostle Paul calls this same Gospel a Gospel of the Son of **God**. In the final time, there shall be the Theology of the Holy Spirit, which John calls an "Everlasting Gospel": which an angel shall proclaim to those who sit and dwell upon the earth; more concerning this will follow below in its proper place. <note>The author is referencing Revelation 14:6, interpreting it as a future revelation of spiritual truth specific to the era of the Holy Spirit.</note>

However, I must remind the reader here that, although (as recounted) three Theologies or Religions will follow one another, none of them is contrary to the others.

<margin>Rom. 10:4.</margin>
For when it is said that Christ is the end of the Law, it is not to be understood thereby <note>The text cuts off here, likely continuing to explain that Christ "fulfills" rather than "destroys" the Law, maintaining the continuity between the three ages.</note>

## The Fourteenth Chapter.

### Three Ways of Understanding the Holy Scripture.

<image-desc>A square decorative woodcut border containing the capital letter "I", featuring intricate acanthus leaf and floral scrollwork.</image-desc> In the time of the Father, 
and thus in the Old 
Testament, there was 
**moral understanding** <note>original: "Intellectus moralis." This refers to reading the Bible as a guide for ethical behavior and law.</note>: for 
there, Theology was 
interpreted and under- 
stood according to such understanding; from which one namely learned how to lead a fine outward 
<margin>Deuteronomy 11:2</margin>life. This was called a discipline of God the Lord. In the time of the Son, and thus in the New Testament, Theology was very much and almost mostly directed toward the **allegorical understanding** <note>original: "intellectum allegoricum." This method of interpretation looks for hidden symbolic meanings, often seeing Old Testament events as "types" or foreshadows of Christ.</note>. 
Of this, David already prophesied in the Old Testament, where he speaks in the person of the Lord 
<margin>Psalm 78:2</margin>Christ: *I will open my mouth in parables, and will utter the secrets from the beginning of the world.* For the Lord Christ presented his entire teaching in 
<margin>Matthew 13:34, Mark 4:33</margin>parables <note>original: "Gleichnüssen," which can mean parables, similes, or metaphors.</note> to his listeners; as the Evangelists clearly testify. In the coming and final **Age** <term>Age</term> <note>original: "Seculo," used here in the theological sense of a distinct era in salvation history.</note>, however, and thus in the time of the Holy Spirit, Theology will be directed toward the **anagogical** <note>Anagogical interpretation seeks the spiritual or mystical sense of scripture, particularly regarding the afterlife, the end of the world, or the soul's ascent to God.</note> or **mystical understanding** <note>original: "Anagogicum oder Mysticum intellectum."</note>. For then all the secrets of the Scripture will become manifest, which until now have been hidden from the eyes of our hearts or minds: Because al-

## The Twentieth Chapter.

### Three Types of Sacrifice.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial letter 'I' at the start of the chapter, featuring ornate foliage and scrollwork designs within a square frame.</image-desc>
<margin>Leviticus & Numbers, in various places. <note>The Latin *passim* indicates that these themes appear throughout the cited books.</note></margin>
IN the time of the Father in the Old Testament, the sacrifice consisted of the blood of goats and calves, and of the fat of animals. Concerning the time of the Son—and thus the sacrifice of the New Testament—prophecies were already made in the Old Testament. First, through the Prophet
<margin>Psalm 4:6.</margin>
David, where he says: Offer the sacrifice of righteousness, and hope in the Lord. This same sacrifice of righteousness is now explained somewhat more clearly concerning Christ by the Prophet Isaiah:
<margin>Isaiah 53:7.</margin>
> He was offered because he himself willed it, and he did not open his mouth; as a sheep he is led to the slaughter. <note>original Latin: *Oblatus est quia ipse voluit & non aperuit os suum : sicut ovis ad occisionem ducitur.*</note>

He was offered up because it pleased him to do so: And he did not open his mouth, like a lamb being led to the slaughtering-bench. But that Christ is indeed such a sacrifice of the New Testament is first testified by the
<margin>Ephesians 5:2.</margin>
Apostle Paul, saying: Christ gave himself for us as a gift and sacrifice, a sweet-smelling aroma to GOD. And
<margin>1 Peter 2:24.</margin>
Peter says: Christ himself offered up our sins in his own body upon the wood <note>A common historical reference to the wooden Cross.</note>: so that we, having died to sin, might live for righteousness. Elsewhere, too, an express distinction is made between the first
<margin>Hebrews 10:9, 10.</margin>
and second sacrifice: and the sacrifice of the body of Jesus Christ is praised.

Regarding the sacrifice of the third and final time, prophecies were likewise already made in the Old Testament

## The Twenty-Fourth Chapter.

# Three-fold Assembly of the Elect.

<image-desc>Ornate decorative woodcut initial 'E' featuring scrolling foliage and floral patterns within a square border.</image-desc>
Furthermore, according to the three ages, there are also three types of assemblies of the Elect, together with the community of all their goods; in this, one age is likewise always a precursor <note>literally "pre-image" or "type"</note> to the others. 
<margin>Genesis 7</margin>
First, in the *Age of the Father* <note>original Latin: "Seculo Patris"</note> of the Old Testament, only those persons were gathered in *Noah’s Ark* whom God the Lord wished to preserve and from whom He wished to build a church. All others who were outside that assembly had to drown and perish. So also there is no doubt that Noah held everything in common with his children in the Ark, just as every head of a household <term>Hauß-vatter</term> <note>refers to the traditional role of the patriarch managing a shared family estate</note> is otherwise accustomed to do in his own home. 
<margin>Acts 2:1</margin>
Afterward, in the time of the Son and the New Testament, the disciples of Christ were all together with one accord in one house. And these were, at that time, the very ones whom God had chosen for His church. 
<margin>Gregory, Morals, book 35 <note>referring to the "Moralia in Job" by Pope Gregory the Great</note></margin>
All others, who would not accept the previously promised and now-sent Jesus Christ, were rejected by God and remained under God’s wrath; hence it is said: *Outside the Church there is no salvation* <note>original Latin: "Extra Ecclesia non est salus"</note>; that is, outside of the universal assembly there is no healing. 
<margin>Acts 2:44</margin>
Thus, the disciples of Christ and the Christian brothers also held all things in common in such an assembly and fellowship of persons...

held all goods in common; all as a prototype <note>original: "vorbilde," meaning a model or prophetic precursor</note> of the community of persons and goods that will exist among all the Elect of God in the future world. For in the **third and final age** <note>original: "zeit," here referring to the Joachimite concept of the *Seculum* or Age of the Spirit</note> **of the Holy Spirit**, all of God's Elect, together with all holy angels, will be gathered in the **New Heavenly Jerusalem**. David already prophesied in the Spirit concerning such a gathering <margin>Psalm 122:3</margin> with these words: *Jerusalem is built so that it may be a city where people shall come together.* And the Lord Christ <margin>John 10:16</margin> also proclaimed this when He said: *There shall be one flock and one shepherd.* Likewise, <margin>Romans 12:4–5; 1 Corinthians 12:18; Ephesians 4:2–3; Colossians 2:19</margin> Paul discusses this harmonious assembly of Christ with His Elect here and there throughout his Epistles.

Thus, "**Mine and Thine**" <note>original Latin: "Meum vnd Tuum," referring to the concept of private property</note>—which until now has incited all strife, discord, and war in the world—will cease; and no one shall then possess anything as their own alone.

## The Twenty-First Chapter.

### Three Kinds of Songs of Praise of the Holy Angels.

<margin>Isaiah 6:3.</margin>
<margin>Luke 2:14.</margin>
One finds in the Holy Scripture that the holy angels have sung a particular song of praise in every age <note>original: "Seculo," referring to the historical-theological epochs of the Father, Son, and Spirit</note>. First, in the time of the Father in the Old Testament, the Prophet Isaiah heard the Seraphim <note>the highest order of angels, described as having six wings</note> singing this song of praise to one another: Holy, Holy, Holy is the LORD of Sabaoth <note>original: "Zebaoth," a Hebrew title meaning "Lord of Hosts"</note>; all the lands are full of His glory. In the New Testament, in the times of the Son, when He was born into the world, the multitude of the heavenly hosts praised God the Lord with this song of praise: Glory be to God in the highest, peace on earth, and goodwill toward men.

<margin>Revelation 5:11, 12, 13, 14.</margin>
In the third and final age, all the angels (as it was revealed to John in a vision) will fall on their faces before the throne of God, worship God, and sing praise thus: Amen, praise, and glory, and wisdom, and thanks, and honor, and power, and strength be to our God from eternity to eternity, Amen.

Now, in these three angelic songs of praise, according to the <term>Cabalistic</term> <note>referring to Christian Kabbalah, a mystical method of interpreting Scripture to find hidden meanings</note> method: in the first, the Holy Trinity of God is expressed; in the second, the <term>Harmony</term> <note>original: "Harmonia," meaning a concordant unity or cosmic resonance</note> or concordant unity of God, the macrocosm <note>original: "grossen... Welt," the great world or universe</note> and the microcosm <note>original: "kleinen Welt," the little world or the individual human</note>, as well as likewise the occasion of the

### The Twenty-Sixth Chapter.

<image-desc>Large ornamental woodcut initial letter 'A' decorated with botanical scrollwork and patterns.</image-desc>

<margin>Deuteronomy 31, 32.</margin>
praise which Moses left to the children of Israel at the end of his life: which he received from God Himself and commanded the entire Church and assembly of the Old Testament to sing. ## The Twenty-Seventh Chapter.

### Three Kinds of Cities of Jerusalem.

<margin>2 Samuel 5, 6, 7 <note>The source cites "2 Reg." (Kings), which in older Bibles refers to the books of Samuel.</note></margin>
<margin>Luke 19:41 and the following.</margin>
In the first age, the Age of the Father, there existed the city of Jerusalem which King David captured, inhabited, and expanded: this city was afterwards completely and utterly destroyed to the ground, just as had been previously prophesied to her by Christ.

<margin>2 Peter 3.</margin>

In the third and final age, the third and New Jerusalem shall be let down by God out of heaven, just as was shown to John in his vision <note>original: "Gesichte," referring to the divine revelations in the Book of Revelation</note>.

# The Twenty-Eighth Chapter.

## Three Kinds of Gifts that the Kings Bring to Jerusalem.

<margin>
1 Kings 4:34 <note>The source cites "3 Reg.", which refers to the Third Book of Kings in older Bibles, known today as 1 Kings.</note>
2 Chronicles 9:9, 13, 14, 24 <note>The source cites "2 Par.", an abbreviation for 2 Paralipomenon, the traditional name for the Book of Chronicles.</note>
</margin>
<margin>
Matthew 2:11, and following.
</margin>
<margin>

</margin>
<margin>

Psalm 72:10, 11.
</margin>
In the time of the Father in the Old Testament, the pagan kings and queens from the realm of Arabia and other places came to King Solomon in Jerusalem and brought him gold, spices, and other gifts. In the time of the Son in the New Testament, the Wise Men—or as some would have it, Kings—from the East also came to Jerusalem to visit the newborn infant Jesus, the King of the Jews. And because they did not find Him there, they followed after Him further as far as Bethlehem, where He had been born, and there they also presented Him with gold, frankincense, and myrrh. Both of these events have occurred as a prefiguration <note>original: "vorbilde," a historical event that foreshadows a future spiritual reality</note> of that which is yet to happen in the future. For in the third, future, and final age, the kings of the earth shall bring their glory into the New Jerusalem; just as all this was previously prophesied by the prophet, stating that they shall all come from Sheba <note>original: "Saba"</note>, bringing gold and frankincense.

## The Twenty-Ninth Chapter.

# Three Kinds of Temples in Jerusalem.

<margin>
1 Kings 6, etc. <note>original: "3 Regum," the Third Book of Kings, which is 1 Kings in modern Bibles.</note>
2 Chronicles 5 <note>original: "2 Paral.," an abbreviation for Paralipomenon, the traditional name for the Books of Chronicles.</note>
Josephus, *Antiquities of the Jews*, book 10, chapter 1.
</margin>
Just as there are three kinds of cities of Jerusalem according to the three main ages, there are likewise three kinds of temples for these same cities. In the first age, there was the temple that King Solomon built in Jerusalem out of wood, stones, and metals. This same temple stood for 470 years <note>The chronology of the First Temple's duration varied among early modern scholars; 470 years was a common calculation from its completion to the Babylonian Captivity.</note> and was afterwards burned down by the commander <note>The Babylonian general Nebuzaradan, acting for King Nebuchadnezzar.</note> of the King of Babylon.

<margin>
John 2:18, 19, 21.
Matthew 24:1, 2.
Luke 21:5, 6, and following.
Revelation 21:22.
</margin>
In the second age of the Son, the temple was the body of Christ, of which Christ said to the Jews who demanded a sign from Him: they should tear down this temple (but He meant His body) and He would raise it up again on the third day. Or, if one wishes to understand it as a physical building, the second temple was the one built under Zerubbabel <note>The leader of the Jewish people who oversaw the rebuilding of the Temple after the Babylonian exile.</note> and destroyed by Titus <note>The Roman Emperor who, as a general in 70 AD, destroyed the Second Temple.</note>: of which Christ had previously and clearly prophesied. In the third and final age of the Holy Spirit, the third temple in the third and New Jerusalem will be God the **LORD** Himself, as it is prophesied that there will be no other temple within it, but the Lord, the almighty God, and the Lamb shall be their temple. With this, the Talmudists <note>Jewish scholars of the Talmud; the author suggests that Jewish tradition's focus on only two physical temples aligns with his spiritual interpretation of the third.</note> agree to some extent, as they also write that only two temples will be built in this world.

### The thirty-first Chapter.

### Three-fold Kinds of Government.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial letter 'O' featuring stylized scrollwork and floral motifs within a circular frame, set inside a square border.</image-desc>

Although these things—which we set down one after another in several following chapters—cannot be proven from God’s Word as clearly and obviously as those that preceded them, considering that they are not exactly the kind of things that properly belong to spiritual matters or the Word of God. For many of them concern civil governance <note>The original German uses the word *Policey*, which in this era refers to the general administration, policy, and order of a state, rather than just the modern police force.</note> and the liberal arts <note>The *Freye Künste*, or Seven Liberal Arts (Grammar, Logic, Rhetoric, Arithmetic, Geometry, Music, and Astronomy), were the foundation of a classical university education.</note> more directly. Nevertheless, we will endeavor to write of them that which can, in part, be known with certainty and doubtlessly to be true in itself; but also that which at least to me (the author of the present treatise <note>original: *Authori gegenwertigen Tractatus*</note>) is strongly presumed, if not entirely certain, based on well-founded indications. In this, I leave it entirely to the Christian reader—especially concerning that which is described and prophesied here regarding the future and final age—to give their belief and assent, or to hold another opinion on the matter.

Three forms of the state <note>original Latin: *Formæ Reipublicæ*</note> or kinds of government are primarily described by the philosophers: namely, **Monarchy**, **Aristocracy**, and **Democracy**. Now we wish to set forth how these three kinds have been in practice <note>original German: *im schwang gegangen*—meaning to be in use or prevailing.</note> according to the three aforementioned chief eras, and how they still [continue]

In the time of the Son, Monarchy and Aristocracy <term>Aristocratia: government by the nobility or a privileged ruling class</term> have flourished together: where namely the highest authority, such as the Roman Emperor, conducts the government together with the foremost Estates <note>The "Estates" (Stände) refers to the social and political orders of the Holy Roman Empire—such as princes, counts, and prelates—who shared power with the Emperor.</note> of the Empire, which indeed still continues today. In the age of the Father, Empirical Medicine <term>Empirical Medicine: A system of healing based strictly on observation and past experience rather than underlying theories or causes.</term> was in use, which was directed solely toward certain experiments or experiences / namely, where those things which had once helped against a disease were later noted; and these same treatments were prescribed for other people in similar illnesses: And this [method] is said to have been first invented by Aesculapius <note>The ancient Greek hero and god of medicine.</note> in this way: that in his travels here and there he brought together all kinds of medicinal experiments / and afterwards cured people with them. # The Thirty-Fifth Chapter.
## Threefold Philosophy.

<image-desc>An ornamental woodcut initial "I" featuring scrolling foliage and floral motifs within a square frame.</image-desc>
In the time of the Father, the Chaldean, Persian, and Egyptian philosophy flourished: To this may also be added the philosophy of Plato, because it is quite comparable to those others / and he is said to have learned such things from the Egyptians. In the time of the Son until the present day, Greek and Aristotelian philosophy has been in use, in which the Seven Liberal Arts <term>Seven Liberal Arts: The traditional curriculum of medieval universities, consisting of the Trivium (grammar, logic, and rhetoric) and the Quadrivium (arithmetic, geometry, music, and astronomy).</term> are described: Upon which, in many places today, people unfortunately also wish to ground Theology <note>original: "Theologiam"</note>. In the third and final age, however, another certain, perfect, and constant philosophy, together with the Seven Liberal Arts / will arise in the highest perfection and certainty and remain constant. Mention shall be made of this at length further below.

#### Chapter One.
#### Concerning the twofold coming of Christ.

<image-desc>Ornamental drop cap 'D' decorated with a central vase, floral blooms, and symmetrical vine scrolls.</image-desc>
In order that we may now further prove that which has been established in the First Part regarding the three <term>ages<note>original: "Seculis" (Latin). The author uses this term to describe the distinct epochs or world-eras of divine history.</note></term> or periods—namely, that three distinct times follow one another—we wish to provide a detailed account here from the Word of God concerning the twofold coming of the Lord Christ. Through this, we hope our purpose will be all the more grounded and confirmed. And so much depends upon the distinction between these two comings of the Lord Christ that it is most useful and beneficial for us in reaching a correct understanding of the Prophets.

is. On the other hand, without a distinction and right understanding of these, one can hardly be properly and correctly instructed from the *Weissagungen* <term>prophecies<note>original: "Weissagungen." A term specifically referring to the divinely inspired predictions of the biblical prophets.</note></term> of the Prophets. This is easily seen in the fact that when a *Jew* disputes with a *Christian*, he will show and present many passages from the Prophets which speak of the coming of the *Messiah*; and yet, so much is expressed in them that these passages cannot be understood as referring to anything other than the <note>The "last time" or "end times" in this context refers to the final age of the world before the Last Judgment.</note> last time. Or, perhaps they are such things that were prophesied by the Prophets concerning the *Messiah* which did not occur during *Christ’s* time in this world. From this, a *Jew* would then conclude against a *Christian* that the *Messiah* has not yet come, but is still in the future; and for the reason just mentioned, *Christ Jesus* could not be that same promised *Messiah*.

If now (I say) a *Christian* has no right understanding or instruction regarding the twofold comings of *Christ*, and does not know how to distinguish properly between them, or how to explain their difference or the prophecies of the Prophets concerning the coming of the *Messiah*, he will in this case be caught as if in a *Labyrinth*. He will have to concede the point to the *Jew* and will be unable to answer his objections. 

Conversely, this is the best and most certain method or "master-piece" if one wishes to convert a *Jew* to the *Christian* faith: that one must, above all things, firmly instill in him the nature of these two distinct comings of the *Messiah*, and according to them, one must explain the prophecies of the Prophets. Through this, his error would be all the more easily removed, and he, on the contrary, [would be strengthened] in the faith—

# Second Part

...be properly instructed, and in the same also be all the more strongly confirmed. The Lord Christ himself used this same master-stroke <note>Referring to the method of distinguishing between the two comings of the Messiah.</note> toward the two disciples on the road <margin>Luke 24:17 & following</margin> to Emmaus: since He noticed that they were also burdened with *Judaism* <note>In this 17th-century theological context, "Judaism" refers specifically to the contemporary Jewish expectation of a political, triumphant Messiah rather than a suffering one.</note>, knowing nothing more than of a future Messiah; and were in the false delusion that the Lord Christ, while He walked on earth, should have immediately established His kingdom and redeemed the people of Israel. Against this, Christ, beginning from Moses and all the Prophets, interpreted the Scripture for them: which entire interpretation was primarily directed toward the distinction of the two comings of the Messiah, and finally aimed at the point that the Messiah must first suffer all such things (as had happened) and only then enter into His glory.

Thus, Christ’s coming is of two kinds; the first into the flesh in this present world: the other will be to judge the living and the dead, in the third and last world <note>The "third world" likely refers to the eternal state or the "new heaven and new earth" following the current age and the millennial age/judgment.</note>, as the Scripture testifies in many places concerning this; and there are also two kinds of states of Christ, namely <term>status humiliationis <note>Latin: "state of humiliation."</note></term>, that is, the state of lowering: and <term>Status Gloriæ <note>Latin: "state of glory."</note></term>, that is, the state of Glory. Regarding the first state of Humiliation, in which Christ was to be on earth in His first <margin>Psalm 22. Isaiah 53.</margin> coming, King David as well as the Prophet Isaiah prophesied quite magnificently and clearly; but the Apostle Paul afterwards wrote even more clearly as follows: <margin>Philippians 2:7, 8.</margin> Christ took on the form of a servant, became like any other man, and was found in appearance as a man. He humbled Himself: and became obedient unto death, yes, even unto death

...of the cross. Thus, the Lord Christ, while here in this world—from His birth until His death—conducted Himself in such a way that His divine nature and essence were, for the most part, not outwardly or publicly perceived. Even when He, while here on earth, raised the dead, made the lepers clean, the blind seeing, the deaf hearing, and the mute speaking: such things occurred at that time before the eyes of men only in a physical manner, for the sake of their physical life’s-salvation and health, so long as their temporary life in this world endured. Just as the prophet Isaiah previously prophesied, it also came to pass that many were offended by such a lowly state of the Lord Christ—not only among the Jews, but even to this day among many who carry the Christian name. Among these, for instance, are the Arians <note>A group following the teachings of Arius (c. 256–336 AD), who argued that Jesus was a created being and not co-eternal or "of one substance" with God the Father.</note>, who will not admit or even believe:

## The Second Chapter.

### Concerning the second coming of Christ.

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial 'N' containing foliate and vine motifs, marking the start of the chapter.</image-desc>

**NOW**, concerning this second coming of the Lord **CHRIST**, which we still await, it is of the utmost importance to us. Indeed, His first coming—His suffering, death, resurrection, descent into hell, and ascension to heaven—would all be worth less than nothing to us if He (the Lord **CHRIST**) did not return a second time and 
<margin>John 14:3.</margin>
take us to Himself, so that we might be where He is, just as He promised for the comfort of His disciples. Yes, the entire Gospel of the New Testament was primarily and most of all, and still is, a sermon and proclamation of this second coming of the Messiah <note>original: *Messie*</note> and of the Last Judgment. 
<margin>Acts 10:42.</margin>

<margin>Chapter 17:30, 31.</margin>

## The Third Chapter.

### Concerning the State of Humiliation and also the Glory of Christ.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut drop cap 'O' featuring stylized foliage and floral scrollwork within a square frame.</image-desc>
Although these things have already been considered above / and each State <note>The "States of Christ" (status Christi) is a theological concept divided into the "State of Humiliation" (his earthly life and suffering) and the "State of Exaltation" or "Glory" (his resurrection and reign).</note> has been proven in particular from **God's** Word: yet we wish to cite here in this chapter two additional glorious testimonies of the Apostle Peter, in each of which both of these states of Christ are mentioned together.

<margin>1 Pet. 1:11, 12. & Chap. 5:1.</margin>
And first he writes: That the Spirit of Christ / who was in the prophets / has testified to the sufferings (behold the state of humiliation!) <note>original Latin: *ecce statum humiliationis!*</note> that are in Christ / and the glory <note>The original German uses *Herzlichkeit* (heartiness/sincerity), which in this period was an interchangeable variant for *Herrlichkeit* (glory/splendor), as confirmed by the accompanying Latin phrase.</note> thereafter (behold the state of glory) <note>original Latin: *ecce statum gloriæ*</note> to whom it is revealed: For they have performed it not for themselves / but for us.

<margin>1 Pet. 4:12.</margin>
And again afterward: Rejoice that you suffer with Christ: So that at the time of the revelation of his glory you may also have joy and delight.

<margin>Phil. 2:7, 8, 9.</margin>
Just as the Apostle Paul no less mentions both these states of Christ together in one place.

However, if one looks at our present time, one will find that since the beginning of Christ’s first coming, there have never before appeared so many different <term>factions</term> <note>original: "Rotten," a derogatory term for disorderly groups or mobs</note>, sects, and erroneous opinions in religion—even if one were to add them all together—as have emerged within living memory throughout Christendom. ...lasted long in one place one after another. On the other hand, let one look at the current wars; have some of them not lasted nearly thirty years now? And furthermore, some of these, such as those in the Netherlands and France, are **civil wars** <note>original: "intestina bella"</note> or internal wars, which are especially grievous conflicts. These originated primarily from **religious disputes** <note>original: "Religions-streit"</note>—not without a special premonition of the end of this world. Just as also within a few years in Germany, several dangerous examples have arisen and a new state of affairs has emerged, of which no one can yet know how far it might spread. And it is quite possible that the priest **Esdras** <note>Ezra, specifically referring to the apocryphal book 4 Esdras (2 Esdras in many English Bibles), which was often cited in apocalyptic literature.</note> intended to indicate such internal

<margin>4. Times of Scarcity.</margin>
The fourth sign is **expensive times** <term>expensive times: "theure Zeit" refers to periods of high inflation, famine, or scarcity where the cost of living becomes unbearable</term>. Whereas in ancient times, perhaps in rare instances, there was a famine in a land for one year, it usually arose from a **crop failure** <note>original: "Mißwachs"</note> of the grain and the fruits and a great lack thereof: and it ceased afterward as soon as the crops grew again in the following year; and it became as **affordable** <note>original: "wolfeil"</note> as it had been before. But look at the current state and circumstances of the time, when all kinds of grain and fruit—even in those years when they should be very cheap—must be bought two or three times as dearly as happened 50, 60, or more years ago. Beyond this, such high prices last in these times without ceasing from one year to the next;

This event was not confined to a single city or region, nor even to a single country, but was felt across various kingdoms, principalities, lands, and cities—specifically in Hungary, Bohemia, Austria, Moravia <note>original: "Meheren"</note>, Silesia, Meissen, and many other lands. # Second Part

<margin>8. Contempt, hatred, and envy among Christians.</margin>
The eighth sign is that even among Christians, many will betray and hate one another. Now, let anyone look at the current state of affairs in almost all Christian kingdoms and lands—in cities and governments, among the clergy and the laity, and among persons of both high and low status. Observe what public or sometimes secret envy, hostility, misunderstanding, and betrayal has been sensed for some time now—and still prevails daily—among people who both claim to be called Christians, yet differ in their Christian faith and various religions. This is even frequently seen among colleagues who belong together, who must live, work, and interact with one another every day in the same city, government, or church! Certainly, such a thing would not easily have been heard of in Christendom in earlier times.

These very people, through their false prophecies, cause and bring about such heavy, long-lasting, and dangerous wars in Christendom. They do this not only by presumptuously persuading their lords and kings that they are acting in a Christian, right, and good manner, but also by daring to falsely prophesy to them—without any basis and against the clear Word of God and previously established, steadfast prophecies—that they shall yet become monarchs or sole rulers of the whole world. 

I will say nothing now of those who, through public printing, dare to presumptuously prophesy to our people such a certain victory against the Turks <note>Referring to the Ottoman Empire</note>, the destruction of that empire, and the reconquest of the Greek Empire <note>The former Byzantine Empire</note>. Not only is it cried out daily by preachers from public pulpits <note>original: "Cantzel"</note>, but it is also spoken of and lamented openly everywhere in the streets. For everyone who loves the truth must admit without contradiction: that all kinds of coarse vices, abominable great un-Christian sins, and shames—in these our final times, in all places—are much more frequent and widespread than they ever were in antiquity, or than we have ever read of in histories <note>original: "Historien"</note>. They are so much in fashion and practiced so shamelessly that people often do not even consider them punishable or wrong.

As for the other injustice which may be practiced today in princely governments, chancelleries, and the courts of the nobility, the countryside, the cities, and the villages—as well as in administrative offices and all other similar judicial posts—I do not wish to tax <note>In this context, to accuse or charge</note>, punish, or judge anyone specifically here (since this treatise <note>original: "Tractat"</note> was not written for that purpose). However, let each person examine themselves in this regard: whether they always grant the same right and impartial justice in their office <note>original: "Ampt"</note> to the poor as to the rich, to the lowly as to the powerful, and to the enemy as to the friend, without any personal feelings, favor, gifts, bribes, or other sought-after benefits. Likewise, let each private person consider how they behave toward their neighbor <term>original: "Negsten" — the fellow human being to whom one has a moral and biblical duty</term> in their own station, trade, business, and calling, and how they preserve their conscience <note>original: "Gewissen"</note>. When one now considers the state of this present time in this regard compared to the conditions of previous times, one will certainly find in practice that Christian love among the great masses has grown cold—indeed, has completely and utterly frozen. For even though the Lord Christ left us this commandment as a farewell <note>original: "valet" — a parting gift or instruction</note>—that we should love one another—it has (alas) come to pass in these final times that a person is very rarely found who (except for close blood relation, hoped-for gain, or repayment, fame, and praise) would show his neighbor righteous Christian love and brotherly faithfulness, or generously practice works of mercy <note>original: "barmhertzigkeit"</note> toward the needy. This immoderate cooling of brotherly love and un-Christian hardening of human hearts has, only within human memory, so greatly and extremely taken the upper hand that in ancient times such lack of charity and mercilessness was not easily heard of in such a manner. Rather, at that time, it would have been considered a great and un-Christian sin. Yet, unfortunately, among us now, it has become an ill-introduced, now long-standing custom (indeed, a misuse <note>original: "mißbrauch"</note>) and a wicked habit.

<margin>II. Moses.</margin>The other good person who is to come before the Day of the Lord is Moses. # Second Part.

...also nearly 1,600 years have already passed; to say nothing of the fact that, according to the movements of the heavens, the Great Conjunction—which began in the Year of Christ 1583 at the end of the Watery Trigon and the beginning of the Fiery [Trigon]—will fully run its course by the Year of Christ 1642. <note>In historical astrology, a "Great Conjunction" refers to the alignment of the planets Jupiter and Saturn. These were believed to signal massive shifts in history, religion, and empires, especially when they transitioned between the four "trigons" or groups of zodiac signs associated with the elements (Water, Fire, Earth, Air).</note>

<margin><unclear>7, 11, 20 gA</unclear></margin>

<margin>Matthew 24:36</margin>
concerning the day and the hour when the **Lord** shall actually come, no one knows, not even the angels in heaven, nor even the Son, but 

<margin>Acts 1:7</margin>
only the Father, who has reserved such things for His own power. Therefore, it is rightly to be considered a great presumption that people have recently appeared who, through the cleverness of their own reason, wish to calculate in which year, or even on which day, the Day of the **Lord** 

<margin>Ecclesiastes 9:12</margin>
shall come! Since there is no human who could know the day and hour of their own end or natural death, how then could one know in advance the certain time and hour of the destruction of heaven and earth, and thus the certain arrival of the Last Judgment? <margin>Augustine, Letter 73. <note>original: "Augustin. Epist. 73."</note></margin>

# Second Part.

### The Eighth Chapter.
## Of the Day of the Lord.

<margin>Hebrews 4:3, 4, to 9</margin>
There is no doubt that the great Day of the **Lord**, which is to come, was signified and prefigured by the Seventh Day, which God the **Lord** appointed and instituted as a Sabbath and day of rest to be kept and celebrated after the six workdays. This can be understood in part from Holy Scripture, but especially from these words (which are hidden and incomprehensible to many): *Therefore there remains a Sabbath rest for the people of God.* <note>original Latin: "Itaque relinquitur Sabbatismus populo Dei."</note> For just as there are six workdays in the week, and the Sabbath follows after them: so too will the world, according to the prophecy of Elijah, <note>The "Prophecy of Elijah" is a traditional Jewish and early Christian chronological tradition which teaches that the world will exist for 6,000 years (six "days" in God's sight) before the final Sabbath or Messianic age.</note> stand for six thousand years—that is, six great days—during which humans on Earth must eat their bread in the sweat of their brow and seek and earn their sustenance with great toil and labor.
<margin>Isaiah 14:7</margin>
But after the passing of those same six great workdays, the great Sabbath and day of rest of the **Lord** shall begin; therein (as the Prophet says), the whole world will rest, be still, and shout for joy.

<margin>Isaiah 13:9</margin>
This Day, however, is now described in Scripture for the most part as terrifying! For thus says the Prophet Isaiah: Behold, the Day of the **Lord** comes, cruel, with wrath and fierce anger.
<margin>Joel 2:11</margin>
So also speaks the Prophet Joel: The Day of the **Lord** is great and very terrible; who can endure it?

<summary>This page explains the theological concept of the "Day of the Lord" as an eternal Sabbath, mirroring the seven-day creation week through a 6,000-year timeline of human history. It warns that while this day brings rest for the world, the biblical prophets describe its arrival as a time of immense terror and divine wrath.</summary>

<keywords>Day of the Lord, Sabbath rest, prophecy of Elijah, six thousand years, Isaiah, Joel, Day of Judgment, workdays</keywords>

<header>Second Part.</header>

## The Ninth Chapter.

## Of the Thousand Years, of which mention is made in the Revelation of John.

<margin>Isaiah 24:21, 22.</margin>
The prophet Isaiah speaks thus: At that time the Lord will punish the host of the high ones that are on high, and the kings of the earth: so that they shall be gathered together in a bundle <note>German: *bündlein*. A reference to being bound or gathered for judgment.</note> in the pit, and shall be shut up in the prison, and after a long time shall be visited again.

<margin>Revelation 20:1, 2, & seq.</margin>
Concerning this, this Revelation now comes to John: That an angel shall come down from heaven: And shall bind the dragon, that old serpent, which is the devil and Satan, for a thousand years: And shall cast him into the abyss, shut him up, and set a seal upon him; until the thousand years should be fulfilled. Likewise, that the elect shall reign with Christ for a thousand years: and when the thousand years are expired, Satan shall be loosed again out of his prison. Concerning these thousand years now—when they shall begin, and what else is indicated thereby—there are many different opinions of the learned, who, from the number <term>chilia <note>Greek: *chilia* (χίλια), meaning "a thousand."</note></term> (thousand), are called <term>Chiliasts <note>Also known as Millenarians; people who believe in a literal thousand-year period of peace and the reign of Christ on earth.</note></term>, which we consider unnecessary to recount here at length: we also have various reservations about judging them for certain reasons. We wish only to explain briefly what our opinion is on this matter: but we do not wish to force anyone to accept this or to give their assent to it,

This calculation implies that time in the spiritual or prophetic sense operates on a different scale than human time.</note> That so much is mentioned in the **Scriptures** about the **Day of the Lord** would therefore not be so absurd if one understood these thousand years as being that very Day of the Lord. For, according to the explanation of Scripture, one day with **God** is a thousand years, <margin>Psalm 90:4.</margin> as **David** says: *For a thousand years are before you as the day of yesterday which has passed, and like a watch in the night.* The **Apostle Peter** also writes in the very place where he reports on the coming and the Day of the Lord Christ: <margin>2 Peter 3:8.</margin> *But let this one thing not be hidden from you, dear brothers: That one day before the Lord is as a thousand years, and a thousand years as one day.* However, these thousand-year days will not be distinguished by individual days and nights (as we now have in this world), but will be one single continuous day.
