# Second Part.
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<margin>Augustine, on the First Epistle of John, Tractate 4.</margin>
(the **Lord Christ’s**) first coming <note>original: "zukunfft"</note> was so that He should be **judged**: but the second coming is so that He should **judge**. Just as previously, in the <term>state of humiliation<gloss>A theological concept referring to the period of Christ's earthly life where He set aside His divine glory to live as a human.</gloss></term>, the **Lord Christ** was judged in the flesh: so also will He, in His other coming, judge in the **Spirit** and through the **Spirit of God**, of which the prophet **Isaiah** prophesies 

<margin>Isaiah 4:4</margin>
thus: That the **Lord** will wash the filth of the daughter of **Zion**, and drive the blood-guilt of **Jerusalem** from her, through the **Spirit**, who will judge and kindle a fire.

<margin>Chapter 28:6</margin>
Likewise: At that time the **Lord <term>Sabaoth<gloss>A Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord of Hosts."</gloss></term>** will be a **Spirit of Justice** to him who sits in 

<margin>Chapter 42:1 etc.</margin>
judgment. And again: Behold, this is my **servant**, I uphold him, and my **chosen one**, in whom my soul has a delight! I have given him my **Spirit**; he will bring judgment among the **heathens**. So 

<margin>2 Esdras 12:32, 33</margin>
it is also said in the vision to **Ezra**: The **Spirit**, whom the **Most High** keeps with him until the end, he will then convict the people because of their godlessness, and reveal their wickedness to them: For he will set them alive before judgment, and punish them after sufficient conviction. Toward this the **Lord Christ** himself looked, when he prophesied of the **Holy Spirit’s Office**, when he should 

<margin>John 16:8</margin>
come on earth, saying: When the **Comforter** comes, he will punish the world for **sin**, and for **righteousness**, and for **judgment**.

<summary>This page explores the dual nature of Christ's mission, contrasting His first appearance as one who was judged with His future return as the supreme Judge acting through the Holy Spirit. It supports this using various Old Testament prophecies and Christ's own words regarding the role of the Comforter.</summary>

# Second Part.

<margin>Augustine on the First Epistle of John, Tractate 4.</margin>
[The Lord Christ's] first coming was so that he might be judged; but his second coming will be so that he may judge. Just as previously, in the <term>state of humiliation<gloss>A theological term referring to the time Christ lived as a mortal man, setting aside his divine glory.</gloss></term>, the Lord Christ was judged in the flesh: so he will also, in his second coming, judge in the Spirit and through the Spirit of God. Concerning this, the Prophet Isaiah
<margin>Isaiah 4:4</margin>
prophesies thus: That the Lord shall wash away the filth of the daughter of Zion, and shall purge the blood-guilt of Jerusalem from her, through the spirit of judgment and through the spirit of burning <note>The original German says "through the Spirit, which shall judge and kindle a fire."</note>.
<margin>Chapter 28:6</margin>
Likewise: At that time the Lord of Sabaoth <note>original: "Zebaoth," a Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord of Hosts."</note> shall be a spirit of justice to him who sits in judgment.
<margin>Chapter 42:1</margin>
And again: “Behold, this is my servant, I uphold him; and my chosen one, in whom my soul delights! I have given him my spirit; he shall bring judgment to the heathens <note>original: "Heyden," referring to the nations of the world.</note>.” 
<margin>2 Esdras 12:32, 33</margin>
So it is also said in the vision to **Esdras**: The spirit which the Most High has kept for himself until the end, he shall then <term>convict<gloss>In this historical context, "überzeugen" (to convince/convict) carries a legal weight, meaning to prove guilt beyond doubt in a court.</gloss></term> men of their godlessness and reveal their wickedness to them: for he shall set them alive before the judgment and punish them after they have been sufficiently convicted. To this end, the Lord Christ himself looked when he prophesied of the office of the **Holy Spirit** when he should come upon the earth,
<margin>John 16:8</margin>
saying: “When the <term>Comforter<gloss>From the Greek "Parakletos," referring to the Holy Spirit as an advocate or helper.</gloss></term> comes, he will convict the world of sin, and of righteousness, and of judgment.”

## The Sixth Chapter.
### On the New Name of the New Jerusalem.

<margin>Isaiah 62:2</margin>
<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'V' featuring ornate foliate and floral scrollwork in a woodcut style.</image-desc>Concerning the New Jerusalem, the Prophet Isaiah prophesies: That it shall be called by a New Name, which the mouth of the **Lord** shall name. The Prophet Ezekiel, however, afterwards explains this name in his description of the New Jerusalem, stating that it shall be called by a word which <margin>Ezekiel 48:35</margin> in English means as much as: **The LORD is here**: <note>original: "hie ist der HERR." This refers to the Hebrew name *Jehovah Shammah*.</note>
<margin>Jeremiah 3:17</margin> And the Prophet Jeremiah says: It shall be called the Throne of the **Lord**. With this Zechariah also agrees when he <margin>Zechariah 8:3</margin> says: Thus says the **Lord**, I am returned unto Zion, and will dwell in the midst of Jerusalem, and Jerusalem shall be called a City of Truth; and the Mountain of the **Lord**, a Mountain of Holiness. And Baruch also says of such <margin>Baruch 5:4</margin> New Jerusalem: That her name shall be called by **God** forever: Peace, Righteousness, Praise, and Blessedness. In the <margin>Hebrews 12:22</margin> New Testament, however, it is called a City of **God** and the heavenly Jerusalem. Now, although these names are not exactly the same in their sequence of words in our English language, they nevertheless agree in meaning with the first-mentioned name (**The LORD is here**) and all unanimously point to the fact that Jerusalem shall and will be a tabernacle or dwelling <margin>Revelation 21:3</margin> of **GOD** with men: Just as such a name was also revealed to John, and shall be further treated in the following chapter.

## The Seventh Chapter.
# On the New Temple of the New Jerusalem.

<margin>Joel 2:32</margin>
<margin>Psalm 133:3</margin>
<margin>Isaiah 46:13</margin>
<margin>Baruch 5:1–3</margin>
<margin>Haggai 2:9</margin>
<margin>1 Kings 6 <note>original: "3 Reg. 6." In older Bibles, the books of Kings were often numbered 1–4, with 3 Kings corresponding to what we now call 1 Kings.</note></margin>
Concerning the New Jerusalem, Joel prophesies thus: Upon Mount Zion and in Jerusalem there shall be deliverance, as the **Lord** has promised. And David says: Upon Mount Zion the **Lord** promises blessing and life forever and ever. Thereupon **God** also offered comfort through Isaiah, where he says: I will give salvation to Zion, and my glory in Jerusalem. Of which glory Baruch <note>Baruch is a book found in the Apocrypha or Deuterocanonical scriptures, often included in Bibles of this era.</note> speaks thus: Put off, O Jerusalem, the garment of your mourning, and put on the glorious adornment from **God** forever. Put on the robe of the righteousness of **God**, and set the crown of the glory of the Eternal upon your head: **God** will manifest your glory under the whole heaven. Such glory the Prophet Haggai attributes to the Temple of the New Jerusalem, when he says: The glory of this latter house shall be greater than that of the former. Now, it is certainly true that the first temple in Jerusalem, built by King Solomon, was a very glorious and precious work: as may be understood from Holy Scripture. Since the glory of the latter temple is to be much greater still, one might wonder how and what that same great glory must be.

### Third Part.

...and mercy of **God**, revelations have likewise occurred to me (the author of this treatise) from the Spirit of God regarding several of the discussed articles and their great internal mysteries. These are so great and exceedingly abundant that they cannot be fully expressed or described with words. However, to share them as much as possible, we intend in time to put such high revelations and mysteries onto paper for those who will be worthy of them, with stately execution and the proof of the **Word of God**, along with the drafting of such a <term>Symbolic Theology</term> <note>original: "Theologiæ Symbolicæ." This refers to a theology that interprets spiritual truths through symbols and divine "signatures" rather than abstract logic.</note>, which is not spun from human brains at all, but is taken from the clear **Word of GOD**.

Eighthly, the <term>Theology</term> of our time, as it is commonly taught in churches and schools, is largely founded upon pagan <term>Philosophy</term>, in such a manner and way that it is commonly tested and proven by our <term>Theologians</term> according to the <term>dialectical modes of argumentation</term> <note>original Latin: "Modis argumentandi Dialecticis." Dialectics is the formal system of logic used in universities to debate and prove points.</note>. It is presented to the youth in schools, as well as to the listeners in the churches, <term>according to the dialectical method</term> <note>original Latin: "juxta methodum dialecticam."</note> and is also extensively expounded and explained according to the <term>rhetorical types of speaking</term> <note>original Latin: "generibus dicendi Rhetoricis."</note>. Indeed, in this our present <term>Century</term> <note>original Latin: "Seculo."</note> it is undoubtedly held that no one can be a <term>Fundamental Theologian</term> <note>original Latin: "Fundamentalis Theologus." A teacher who is grounded in the essential foundations of the faith.</note> or a grounded teacher of the Holy Scripture unless he has first thoroughly and well studied <term>Dialectic</term> and <term>Rhetoric</term> in the schools, and knows how to properly <term>defend</term>, <term>divide</term>, <term>extend</term>, and <term>apply</term> the articles and pieces belonging to the Christian religion according to those rules. ...as well as through the mercy of God, revelations from the Spirit of God have likewise occurred to me (the *author* of this *treatise*) regarding several of the mentioned articles and their great internal mysteries. This refers to a way of understanding divine truths through symbols, types, and spiritual metaphors rather than abstract logic.</note>, which is not spun from the human brain at all, but rather taken from the clear Word of God.

Eighthly, the **theology** of our time—as it is commonly taught in churches and schools—is largely founded upon pagan **philosophy**, in such a way and manner: that it is commonly tested and proven by our **theologians** according to the **dialectical modes of arguing** <note>original Latin: "modis argumentandi dialecticis." Dialectics is the formal study of logical argument and debate.</note>. It is presented to the youth in schools, as well as to the listeners in the churches, **according to the dialectical method** <note>original Latin: "juxta methodum dialecticam."</note>, and is extensively expounded and explained according to the **rhetorical types of speaking** <note>original Latin: "generibus dicendi rhetoricis."</note>. Thus, in this our present **century** <note>original Latin: "seculo."</note>, it is undoubtedly held that no one can be a **fundamental theologian** <note>original Latin: "fundamentalis theologus."</note> or a well-grounded teacher of Holy Scripture unless he has first thoroughly and well studied his **dialectics** and **rhetoric** in the schools, and knows how to properly **defend, divide, extend, and apply** <note>original Latin: "defendiren, dividiren, extendiren, appliciren." These are the technical steps used in scholastic academic debate to analyze a topic.</note> the articles and pieces belonging to the Christian religion according to those disciplines. ### Third Part.

...as **Thomas Aquinas**, **Albert the Great**, **John Duns Scotus**, and others of their kind have gone even further in this: and in very many articles and doctrines, they have sought to prove, verify, and confirm **Theology** not only in the aforementioned manner from **Sophistical Dialectics** <note>original: "Sophistischen Dialectica." This refers to formal logic and debate techniques often criticized by reformers for being overly complex or deceptive.</note>, but also frequently from the principles of **Aristotelian Physics** <note>original: "Aristotelischen Physica." The natural philosophy of Aristotle which dominated medieval science.</note>.

And this is how it has now (as is undeniable and obvious) come to be with **Theology** today in this present **century** <note>original: "Seculo."</note>: As one sees particularly with the **Jesuits**, that they exert themselves to the highest degree to raise their **disciples** to be more like good **Aristotelian philosophers** and **Ciceronian orators** <note>original: "Oratores Ciceronianos." Followers of the rhetorical style of the Roman statesman Cicero.</note> than righteous **theologians**. Hence, they are also generally (as I personally have experienced this many times and in various places where I disputed with them about **religion**) much better grounded and well-read in **Aristotle** than in Holy Scripture, or even in the **writings of the Fathers** <note>original: "scriptis Patrum." The collective works of the early Christian theologians and bishops.</note>. They perhaps believe that they could never maintain their **religion** as stiffly and as invincibly against their **adversaries** through **God’s** Word as they can through **Aristotelian arguments**.

But with the **Theology** of the coming time, it will be an entirely different situation in this regard. For that theology will not be founded upon a **Pagan philosophy**; on the contrary, righteous, constant, certain, and perfect **philosophy** itself will be founded upon **true theology** <note>original: "veram Theologiam."</note>: so that whoever wishes to be a righteous and true **philosopher** will, before all things, have to be a **fundamental theologian**. Of this same right **philosophy**, the Word of **God** speaks in many places

# Third Part.

...and in the other place, it is also mostly named by the sectarians <note>referring to those who follow specific denominations or "sects" rather than the universal church</note> themselves, according to the names of their masters or teachers. Some, however, are still so arrogant and shameless that they persuade others that their human, groundless, and dubious fictions <note>original: "gedichte"; in this context, it refers to fabricated ideas or intellectual inventions</note> are the clear and pure Word of God. They even defend such things with serious force and dare to use—or rather, misuse—the power of great lords and potentates <term>potentates: monarchs or powerful political rulers</term> for this purpose. This is to say nothing of the fact that many are found today who, in both their writings and their preaching, strive more to use elegant, high, magnificent, and "pulpit-style" <term>Cantzelenische: a derogatory term for overly theatrical or pretentious language used by preachers to impress an audience</term> words. They do this so that they might gain a special fame and reputation among their listeners and other people, rather than speaking the clear, infallible Word of God into the hearts of men with such simplicity as it was spoken by the Holy Spirit through the mouths of holy people, and afterwards set down in writing with true earnestness and zeal.

<margin>Psalm 12:1 & following.</margin>
But has not the highly enlightened man David clearly prophesied about such quarrelsome and discordant behavior of the theologians, as well as their pulpit-style of speaking, and complained heartily to God about it when he says:

> Help, Lord; for the godly have decreased, and the faithful are few among the children of men. Each one speaks useless things and hypocrisy to the other: and they teach out of a double heart <note>The original German "meinigem" or "menigem" refers to a heart that is divided or duplicitous.</note>. The Lord shall root out all hypocrisy and the tongue that speaks proudly: those who say, "Our tongue shall prevail; it is our right to speak. Who is the Lord?" Because then the miserable...

# Chapter Twelve.

<image-desc>Ornamental initial D with floral scrollwork.</image-desc>Mention was made above in the First Part that there are three kinds of understanding of the Holy Scripture; therefore, we wish to explain somewhat more clearly here the third understanding, which will be in force <note>original: "in vigore"</note> in the future time. However, it is first to be remembered here that <margin>Isaiah 45:15.</margin> just as God the Lord is a hidden God, so also is His wisdom secret and hidden; <margin>1 Corinthians 2:7.</margin> that is, His highest and greatest mysteries are not described in Holy Scripture with clear and explicit words, but are hidden therein in a dark manner. <margin>Song of Solomon 4:12.</margin> The Scripture calls such a thing a locked garden, a locked spring, <margin>Revelation 5:1.</margin> and a sealed fountain. Likewise, it is the book sealed with seven seals. <margin>Ezekiel 20:49.</margin> Therefore, the prophet Ezekiel laments: That people accuse him and his teaching as if he speaks nothing but veiled words. ## The Thirteenth Chapter.
### Of the New Book of Holy Scripture.

<image-desc>Decorative ornamental initial 'N' at the start of the first paragraph, featuring a floral or scrollwork background.</image-desc>

After the account of the three types of books of the Holy Scripture—of which mention was made above in the first part, Chapter 15—we will now indicate here in a few words what kind of book of Holy Scripture this shall be, which one will possess in the future and final world. In that earlier section, we have already let it be known that it will be the very book mentioned in Revelation 5:1, the one with the seven seals. However, because the two books of the Old and New Testaments contain their own particular treatises—as the Old Testament contains the five books of Moses, the book of Joshua, the book of Kings and Chronicles, the Psalms of David, the writings of Solomon, the Minor and Major Prophets, along with other individual books; and in the New Testament there are the four Evangelists <note>The four Gospels: Matthew, Mark, Luke, and John</note>, the Acts of the Apostles, along with the Epistles of Paul and several other Apostles—the Book of Revelation, because it treats almost exclusively of the future and final world and its condition, and was also only much later (as is presumed) added to the other books of the New Testament, we shall reckon it more as a book of the Newest Testament <note>original: "Novissimi Testamenti," Latin for the "most recent" or "final" testament, suggesting a third era of revelation</note>. Accordingly, this aforementioned New Book with the seven seals will, in our estimation, contain: first, the just-mentioned Book of the Revelation of John;

<margin>Epistle of Jude v. 14.</margin>
likewise, the book or prophecy of the god-fearing man Enoch, of which the Apostle Jude makes mention; likewise the 70 books <note>A reference to the 70 hidden books mentioned in the apocryphal text 4 Esdras 14:46, intended only for the "wise among the people."</note>

## The Sixteenth Chapter.

### Concerning the New Covenant which God will establish with His Elect.

Earlier, in the First Part, Chapter 10, a detailed account was given of the New Covenant that God the Lord intends to make with His elect in the future and final world.
<margin>1 John 1:3.</margin>

<margin>John 17:22, 23.</margin>
"I have given them the glory that You gave me: that they may be one, even as we are one, I in them and You in me, so that they may be perfect in one; likewise, so that the love with which You love me may be in them, and I in them."

And because His disciples at that time could not understand this great mystery of such a New Covenant, Christ therefore refers to the future and final age <note>original Latin: "Seculum," referring to a long period of time or a world-age</note>, saying:
<margin>Chapter 14:20.</margin>
"On that day you will recognize that I am in the Father, and you in me, and I in you."
<margin>Ezekiel 34:25.</margin>

<margin>Jeremiah 31:33, 34.</margin>

Concerning this, the royal prophet David prophesied: "Your righteousness is an eternal righteousness, and your law is truth." <margin>Psalm 119: 142, 160.</margin> Likewise: "All the laws of your righteousness endure forever." <margin>2 Corinthians 9:9.</margin> And the Apostle Paul says: "God's righteousness remains into eternity."

## The Twenty-First Chapter.

### Concerning a New Philosophy.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial letter O, featuring a central oval shield with a heart and floral or foliate scrollwork surrounding it.</image-desc>

Although the word **Philosophy** <term>Philosophy: Literally "love of wisdom." These (as some believe) are said to have had their origin from the godless **Ham**, **Noah's** youngest son: who is said to have engraved and written these seven arts upon fourteen pillars, seven of which were made of **bronze** <note>original: "Erz"</note> so that they could not be destroyed by water: and seven of **brick**, so that they would not be consumed by fire. What foundation, constancy, and certainty there may be in all aspects of such arts (to say nothing of their usefulness) is clearly shown by the **Philosophers'** <note>original: "Philosophorum"</note> own writings. For whoever reads them will be able to sufficiently perceive from them how entirely contradictory they are in almost all of their mentioned **liberal arts** <note>original: "artibus liberalibus"</note>. But whoever desires a brief, summary report of each such art and its authors should only read the *Declamation* <note>A reference to the famous skeptical work published in 1530.</note> written by the excellently learned man **Henry Cornelius Agrippa** <term>Henry Cornelius Agrippa: A famous 16th-century polymath, physician, and occult philosopher known for his skeptical critique of human knowledge.</term>, *On the Uncertainty and Vanity of the Sciences and the Arts* <note>original: "de incertitudine ac vanitate artium & scientiarum"</note>. # Part Three.

Certainly such quarrelsome, litigious, and doubtful **Philosophy**, along with its crumbling **Liberal Arts** <note>original: "Artibus liberalibus"</note>, will surely have no place in the future, final **Age** <term>Seculo: From the Latin 'saeculum,' referring here to the final age of the world or the afterlife.</term>. Instead, because all other things will be renewed, all **fragmentary knowledge** <note>original: "stückwerck"; a reference to 1 Corinthians 13:10: "when that which is perfect is come, then that which is in part shall be done away."</note> will cease, and the **perfect** will begin, those **Arts** will also be renovated, purged, restored, and made certain and perfect. Simply to prefigure and give an indication of this: we wish to take up these **Seven Liberal Arts** one after another, and briefly reveal our opinion regarding each one specifically (so far as it has been given and permitted to us by God to explain).

<margin>I. New Grammar <note>original: "Nova Grammatica"</note>.</margin>

Likewise: from where do so many different words in so many distinct languages originate? How many of these words are there in total, and what is their certain number? In what manner, and with what words or names, might one name every creature and creation of God so that, in that single word or name, one might perfectly, aptly, and masterfully grasp and indicate every such creature's nature, property, use, and effect? Also, [one will see] how they carry out their course and effects in such correct order and division, according to certain spaces or degrees. Just as in the heavens, according to such order and disposition of the **stars** <note>original: "Geſtirnes"</note>, everything—each and every thing contained in all of Nature—is to be read, seen, and understood, much like writing in a book. 

Indeed, there is also a <term>harmony of the upper world with the lower world</term> <note>original: "harmonia mundi ſuperioris cum mundo inferiore"</note>; that is, how all and every upper and heavenly **body** <note>original: "corpora"</note> unites **harmoniously** <note>original: "harmonicè"</note> with the lower earthly **bodies** <note>original: "corporibus"</note>. Thus, the Heaven joins with the Earth, just as a husband with a wife, to have its union, effect, and birth. 

However, the reason that such great mysteries, which the Lord God wisely ordained into these His creatures, are currently hidden from the world and from almost all men, is this: otherwise, sinful men would use such knowledge for their sinful greed for honor and money, for pride, unchastity, vengefulness, and other [vices]...

<summary>This page describes a future state of perfect astrological knowledge where the stars are understood as living divine instruments that influence the weather, politics, and the human mind. # Third Part.

### The Twenty-Fourth Chapter.

->That there shall be one single religion and one single assembly of the elect.<-

# Part Three.

Churches or assemblies—whether they be called by sectarian names such as the Roman Church, the Greek Church, the Bohemian Church, or the Augustan Church <note>The "Augustan Church" refers to the Lutheran faith, named after the Augsburg Confession (*Confessio Augustana*).</note>—will no longer be heard of; instead, there will be one single Church or Assembly of all God’s elect. Regarding this, Gregory says:
<margin>Gregory, *On Ezekiel*, Homily 15.</margin>
That God speaks of His entire Church all at once, as if He were speaking of a single soul. And elsewhere he writes:
<margin>Book 9, *Moralia*.</margin>
That such a Church or Assembly consists in the unity of the believers, just as a body exists in the unity of its members. So also says Chrysostom:
<margin>Chrysostom, *Commentary on the Epistle to the Galatians*, chapter 1.</margin>
*The name of the Church is a name of consensus and concord* <note>original: "*Ecclesiæ nomen est, nomen consensus ac concordiæ*"</note>; as if he meant to say: Church, or Assembly, signifies nothing other than unanimity and harmony. And Gregory
<margin>Gregory, Book 35, *Moralia*.</margin>
prophesied about this, as it were, when he wrote: That at the end of the world—that is, in the final age—there would be one single Church or Assembly of Jews and Heathens. Which
<margin>Bernard, *On the Song of Songs*, Sermon 79.</margin>
opinion was likewise held by Bernard and Augustine.
<margin>Augustine, Book 2, *Evangelical Epistles*, Book 33.</margin>
Now, concerning such a harmonious Assembly and gathering, God the Lord has offered heartfelt comfort through His Holy Prophets. First, He speaks through Isaiah thus: At that time, the torn and plundered people will bring
<margin>Isaiah 18:7.</margin>
gifts to the Lord of Sabaoth <note>A Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord of Hosts" or "Lord of Armies."</note>, to the place where the name of the Lord of Sabaoth dwells, at Mount Zion. Likewise, the Lord will become known to the Egyptians, and the Egyptians will know the Lord at that time, and will serve Him with sacrifices and grain offerings, and will make vows to the Lord and keep them; and
<margin>Chapter 19, verse 21.</margin>
the Lord will strike the Egyptians and heal them, for they shall be converted to the Lord, and He will hear their pleas and heal them. *And a little later* <note>original: "*Et paulò post*"</note>: At that time, Israel will be a third <note>The German "selbdritte" is an archaic term meaning "one of three," referring here to the biblical prophecy of Israel, Egypt, and Assyria being joined together.</note>...

### Part Three.

[The Apostle Paul] has diligently applied himself to clearly illustrate this mystery to his listeners. For first, he writes to the Corinthians as follows:

<margin>1 Corinthians 12:12, 13, 27, 28.</margin>
> Just as a body is one and yet has many members, but all the members of one body, though they are many, are nonetheless one body: so also is Christ. For we are all baptized into one body by one Spirit. <note>original Latin: "Et paulo post," meaning "And a little later"</note>: You are the body of Christ and members, each according to his part. And God has appointed in the congregation <note>original: "Gemeine," referring to the church or community of believers</note> first the apostles, second the prophets, third the teachers, then the miracle-workers.

And likewise to the

<margin>Ephesians 4:11, 12, 13, 15, 16.</margin>
> Ephesians he writes: He has appointed some as apostles, some as prophets, some as evangelists, some as pastors and teachers, so that the saints may be prepared for the work of the ministry; through which the body of Christ may be built up: until we all come to the same faith and knowledge of the Son of God: and become a perfect man, who is of the measure of the full age of Christ. <note>original Latin: "Et paulo post," meaning "And a little later"</note>: But let us be upright in love, and grow in all things into him who is the head, Christ: from whom the whole body is joined together, and one member hangs upon another through every joint, whereby one provides assistance to the other. And again, we shall be citizens with the saints, and members of God’s household, built upon the foundation of the apostles and prophets, where Jesus Christ is the cornerstone: upon whom the whole building, joined together, grows into a holy temple in the hearts: upon whom we also are built together for a dwelling of God in the Spirit. Likewise he writes to the Colossians as follows: Christ is the head from whom the whole body receives assistance through joints and connections, and is held together: and thus grows to a divine greatness.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the divine order, building on the previous page's mention of the prophet Isaiah and the Book of Revelation. The text begins by completing the word "indicated" (German: "angedeutet") from the previous page's conclusion, transitioning into an analysis of the Apostle Paul's writings on the unity of the body of Christ.</meta>

<header>Part Three.</header>

indicated through the Apostle **Paul** <note>original: "Paulum"</note> when he writes: **The bread that we break, is that not the Participation <note>original: "Participatio"</note> or distribution of the body of CHRIST?** For it is one bread, so we who are many are one body: Since we all <term>Participate</term> <note>original: "Participiren"; to take part in or share in</note> in one bread. It is as if he wanted to say: Just as a loaf of bread is a single and whole thing, and is afterwards broken into many pieces, divided, and distributed to so many different people: So too do all things come from one single thing, which is **GOD** and his Word.

<margin>1 Cor. 12:12</margin>
Afterward he writes again in the same Epistle: Just as a body is one, and yet has many members, but all the members of one body, though they are many, are yet one body, as is also Christ.

With this, Paul intends to give further understanding of what came before: That although there are many different things, they will finally all consist in one thing, in a completely correct order and harmony <term>harmonia</term> <note>a state of agreement or a "fitting together" of parts</note>, from which they originated: Nothing different than how so many various members on a human being are nevertheless only one body.

<margin>Ephes. 1:10</margin>
<margin>1 Cor. 3:21</margin>
That this is the actual meaning of Paul, he explains here and there in his Epistles: But especially in one place, where he considers the restoration <term>instauration</term> <note>original: "instauration"; the act of renewing or re-establishing a former state</note>, which shall happen in the fullness of time <note>original: "plenitudine temporum"</note>, he writes: That all things shall be gathered together in Christ, both that which is in heaven and on earth. And elsewhere: Everything is ours, but we are Christ's, and Christ is God's. Likewise: Christ is the head of every man, but the man is the head of the woman:

<summary>This page uses the metaphors of the Eucharistic bread and the human body to explain how all diverse elements of creation originate from and will eventually return to a single divine unity in God. The author relies heavily on the Pauline Epistles to argue for a universal "restoration" where all things are gathered under Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Apostle Paul, Body of Christ, Communion, Divine Unity, Restoration, Fullness of Time, Harmony, Theology</keywords>

--- Page 309 ---
<meta>This page concludes the theological argument regarding the cosmic order and the ultimate restoration of all things into God. It builds on the previous discussion of the "instauration" (restoration) of the body of Christ, moving from the unity of the Church to the final sovereignty of God the Father over all creation.</meta>

<header>Third Part.</header>

But God is the head of Christ. Likewise, that God has set Christ at his right hand in heaven, above all principalities, authority, power, dominion, and everything that can be named, not only in this world, but also in the world to come: And He has set Him as the 
<margin>Eph. 1:21</margin>
Head of the Community <note>original: "Gemeine"; usually referring to the Church or the body of believers</note> over all things: which is a body, namely the fullness of Him who fulfills all in all. Of this, the Royal Prophet David already prophesied in the Old Testament when he says: You will make him 
<margin>Psalm 8:7</margin>
Lord over the work of your hands: You have put all things under his feet, which has been treated <term>tractiret</term> <note>discussed or handled in writing</note> more extensively above in Chapters 18 and 26 of this Third Part. But when all things shall be thus subject to the Lord Christ, then must be the end, and then he will deliver the Kingdom 
<margin>1 Cor. 15:28</margin>
to God and his Father: then he will abolish all rule, dominion, and power, and then the Son himself will also be subject to Him who subjected all things to him: So that **GOD** may be all in all.

Therefore he says elsewhere fittingly <note>original: "billig"; in this context meaning justly or appropriately</note>, rightly, and 
<margin>Isa. 41:4; Ch. 44:6; Rev. 1:18; Isa. 43:10; Rev. 4:9; Ch. 5:14</margin>
truly: I am the First and the Last, the Beginning and the End, and the A and O <note>Alpha and Omega, the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet</note> says the Lord, who is, and who was, and who is to come, the Almighty: Before me no God was made, and neither shall there be one after me: I am and I live from eternity to eternity.

->End of this Treatise.<-

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut printer's ornament centered at the bottom of the page, featuring a circular motif containing the characters "(o)" flanked by stylized foliate and floral scrollwork.</image-desc>

<summary>This concluding page of the treatise describes the final hierarchy of divine power, where Christ reigns over all earthly and heavenly authorities before ultimately surrendering the kingdom back to God the Father so that God becomes "all in all." It anchors this vision in the prophecies of David and the apocalyptic declarations of the Book of Revelation.</summary>

### A fine
# TREATISE
## BY JULIUS SPERBER,
->Concerning many kinds of wonderful,<-
->partly previously unheard-of,<-
->or even unusual and strange<-
->things, transactions, and matters,<-
->Which have occurred and taken place not only<-
->generally before the end and after the beginning<-
->of every new Great Age <note>original: "Hauptzeit"; refers to significant historical epochs or periods in the divine plan</note>, but especially<-
->during these past hundred years, from the year<-
->1500 until the year 1600.<-
->Together with a summary and brief<-
->indication of a yet future time,<-
->as the final and Golden Age,<-
->and its general circumstances.<-
->Brought to press by<-
->**BENEDICT BAHNSEN.**<-
